The punitive scene is therefore mechanically continuous.","ayah_refs":["73:12","73:13","73:16","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Punitive Closure of Movement and Intake","parent_semantic_invariant":"Punishment closes ordinary passages: limbs cannot move, the throat cannot pass food, and heat cannot be escaped.","parent_surprising_reach":"Fetters, a wrestling hold, a neck grip, an unwholesome meal, molten residue, and war-fire form one system of bodily closure rather than separate punishment images.","key":"B","name":"Food that cannot be swallowed","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A meal enters the mouth but lodges in the throat, turning nourishment into deprivation and pain.","active_motifs":"tasting and taking food (`quranic:root_000934:B001/m01`); obstruction in the throat (`quranic:root_001091:B001/m01`); abstention from food and drink (`quranic:root_000994:B002/m01`); prevention and weaning from a thing (`quranic:root_000994:B003/m01`); painful punishment (`quranic:root_000994:B005/m01`); unwholesome food that cannot be digested (`quranic:root_001619:B003/m01`); felt pain (`quranic:root_000046:B001/m01`); infliction of pain (`quranic:root_000046:B002/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:13 `طَعَامًا/غُصَّةٍ/عَذَابًا/أَلِيمًا`; 73:16 `وَبِيلًا`","synthesis":"Taste initiates an expected nourishment sequence, but obstruction arrests it. Abstention becomes involuntary, the food proves unwholesome, and the failed passage culminates in inflicted and experienced pain.","ayah_refs":["73:13","73:16"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Punitive Closure of Movement and Intake","parent_semantic_invariant":"Punishment closes ordinary passages: limbs cannot move, the throat cannot pass food, and heat cannot be escaped.","parent_surprising_reach":"Fetters, a wrestling hold, a neck grip, an unwholesome meal, molten residue, and war-fire form one system of bodily closure rather than separate punishment images.","key":"C","name":"Enclosure by fire and molten heat","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Fire, molten matter, and extreme heat surround the punished body.","active_motifs":"blazing infernal fire (`quranic:root_000225:B001/m01`); exposure to fire and its heat (`quranic:root_000879:B001/m01`); molten metal, oily dregs, or pus-like residue (`quranic:root_001452:B003/m01`); punishment by severe pain (`quranic:root_000994:B005/m01`); harmful heaviness (`quranic:root_001619:B002/m01`); clamor of fire and war (`quranic:root_001434:B001/m01`); marching in extreme heat (`quranic:root_001434:B002/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `مَهِّلْهُمْ`; 73:12 `جَحِيمًا`; 73:13 `عَذَابًا`; 73:16 `وَبِيلًا`; 73:20 `صَّلَوٰةَ`; surface anchors unavailable for `quranic:root_001434:B001` and `quranic:root_001434:B002` because `م ع ع` is missing from root coverage","synthesis":"The scene combines radiant heat, direct burning, molten residue, and suffocating clamor. Even movement through the environment becomes a march inside heat, completing the closure of escape.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:12","73:13","73:16","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Failure of Earthly, Celestial, and Hydraulic Containment","parent_semantic_invariant":"Structures that normally support, cover, or channel material lose integrity and release what they hold.","parent_surprising_reach":"Mountain collapse, sky rupture, flood, runoff, and hidden erosion are variants of one containment failure across solid, aerial, and liquid media.","key":"C","name":"Water exceeding channels and boundaries","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Heavy rain and imported runoff overwhelm the distinction between channel, land, and enclosing wall.","active_motifs":"an encircling flood (`quranic:root_000957:B002/m01`); heavy rain with large drops (`quranic:root_001619:B001/m01`); rain exceeding ordinary need (`quranic:root_000138:B006/m01`); runoff arriving from another land (`quranic:root_000009:B005/m01`); rain hanging between cloud and earth (`quranic:root_000672:B005/m01`); weather striking earth and vegetation (`quranic:root_000906:B013/m01`); a fortified enclosing wall (`quranic:root_000957:B010/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14,20 `ٱلْأَرْضُ/أَرْضِ`; 73:16 `وَبِيلًا`; 73:19-20 `سَبِيلًا/سَبِيلِ`; 73:20 `ءَاتُ/يَبْتَغُونَ/يَضْرِبُونَ/طَآئِفَةٌ`","synthesis":"Rain descends, runoff arrives laterally, and flood circles the site until ordinary boundaries fail. The enclosing wall supplies the expected defense whose insufficiency makes the inundation a true collapse scene.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:16","73:19","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Failure of Earthly, Celestial, and Hydraulic Containment","parent_semantic_invariant":"Structures that normally support, cover, or channel material lose integrity and release what they hold.","parent_surprising_reach":"Mountain collapse, sky rupture, flood, runoff, and hidden erosion are variants of one containment failure across solid, aerial, and liquid media.","key":"D","name":"Managed flow as the failed baseline","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Water is ordinarily directed through a channel, gathered in a basin, retained at a source, and released into a river.","active_motifs":"a watercourse and the directing of its flow (`quranic:root_000009:B004/m01`); a basin that retains water (`quranic:root_000018:B006/m01`); abundant well or sea water (`quranic:root_001040:B005/m01`); gathered water (`quranic:root_000532:B013/m01`); a river (`quranic:root_001559:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:16,19 `أَخَذْنَٰهُ/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:19-20 `رَبِّهِ/رَبَّكَ`; 73:20 `ءَاتُ/عَلِمَ/ٱلنَّهَارِ`","synthesis":"Channel, basin, source, store, and river define a controlled hydraulic system. That ordinary sequence supplies the baseline against which excess runoff and rupture become failures of containment rather than undifferentiated water imagery.","ayah_refs":["73:16","73:19","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Message Tested by Testimony and Enactment","parent_semantic_invariant":"A communicated claim is tested by witness, enacted fact, and the quality of its recipients’ response.","parent_surprising_reach":"Falsehood appears not only as lying but as a failed charge, deceptive cloth, rumor-induced panic, and fabricated composition.","key":"A","name":"Envoy, carried message, and witness","reading_type":"surface-primary","scene_or_process":"An envoy arrives bearing a message, observes its recipients, and provides knowledgeable testimony.","active_motifs":"dispatch and release (`quranic:root_000563:B001/m01`); messenger and carried message (`quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`); presence with direct observation (`quranic:root_000822:B001/m01`); testimony grounded in knowledge (`quranic:root_000822:B002/m01`); the tongue as a witness (`quranic:root_000822:B005/m01`); a sign that bears witness to time or condition (`quranic:root_000822:B008/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:15 `أَرْسَلْنَا/رَسُولًا/شَٰهِدًا`; 73:16 `ٱلرَّسُولَ`","synthesis":"Dispatch supplies the sender-recipient relation, the messenger carries the content, presence supplies observation, and testimony converts observation into accountable statement. Tongue and external marker extend witnessing from person to instrument and sign.","ayah_refs":["73:15","73:16"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Message Tested by Testimony and Enactment","parent_semantic_invariant":"A communicated claim is tested by witness, enacted fact, and the quality of its recipients’ response.","parent_surprising_reach":"Falsehood appears not only as lying but as a failed charge, deceptive cloth, rumor-induced panic, and fabricated composition.","key":"B","name":"Denial, rumor, and deceptive representation","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Recipients reject the message while false representations circulate and generate disturbance.","active_motifs":"falsehood opposed to truth (`quranic:root_001290:B001/m01`); attributing falsehood to a speaker (`quranic:root_001290:B002/m01`); a deceptive dyed or patterned cloth (`quranic:root_001290:B009/m01`); denial and concealment of truth (`quranic:root_001307:B003/m01`); alarming or false rumor (`quranic:root_000545:B003/m01`); fabricated discourse made without a prior model (`quranic:root_001167:B004/m01`); disobedience to authority (`quranic:root_001022:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ`; 73:14 `تَرْجُفُ`; 73:16 `عَصَىٰ`; 73:17 `كَفَرْتُمْ`; 73:18 `مَفْعُولًا`","synthesis":"The scene distinguishes several failures of representation: direct lying, accusing another of lying, visual deception, fabricated composition, and rumor. Disobedience is the behavioral outcome when that representational refusal is directed at an envoy.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:14","73:16","73:17","73:18"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Illness, Accommodation, and Restorative Care","parent_semantic_invariant":"When health or operative strength fails, care changes the task’s conditions and opens a path from constriction toward relief.","parent_surprising_reach":"Accommodation is materialized through nursing, relapse, infected wounds, reduced movement, breathing room, and a measure fitted to the patient.","key":"A","name":"Illness progressing under care","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A body leaves equilibrium, movement weakens, a wound worsens or relapses, and an attendant works toward recovery.","active_motifs":"departure from bodily health (`quranic:root_001415:B001/m01`); tending a patient (`quranic:root_001415:B003/m01`); weakened action and curtailed movement (`quranic:root_001415:B004/m01`); dimness and loss of clarity (`quranic:root_001415:B005/m01`); illness taking the body (`quranic:root_000018:B007/m01`); relapse of illness or wound (`quranic:root_001096:B004/m01`); a suppurating sore (`quranic:root_000025:B011/m01`); a wound swelling toward corruption (`quranic:root_000138:B004/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14,20 `ٱلْأَرْضُ/أَرْضِ`; 73:16,19 `أَخَذْنَٰهُ/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:20 `مَرْضَىٰ/يَبْتَغُونَ/غَفُورٌ`","synthesis":"The clinical scene distinguishes disease, impaired function, local infection, relapse, and caregiving. Care is an active counter-process, not merely sympathy for an already named patient.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:16","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Prestige, Luxury, and Its Reversal","parent_semantic_invariant":"Social distinction is constructed through comfort, rank, reputation, display, and claims of superiority, then exposed to reversal.","parent_surprising_reach":"Crown, leaders-as-mountains, fame, padded bodily display, dispersal, abasement, and scarcity make luxury a fragile social construction rather than a stable possession.","key":"C","name":"Dispersal and abasement","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A privileged collective loses cohesion, status, and secure abundance under a severe event.","active_motifs":"ostrich-like dispersal and loss of collective strength (`quranic:root_001525:B008/m01`); scarcity and abasement (`quranic:root_001251:B001/m01`); others replacing or following the first (`quranic:root_000019:B001/m01`); a calamitous event (`quranic:root_001700:B003/m01`); harmful consequence (`quranic:root_001619:B002/m01`); baseness and lowered rank (`quranic:root_000493:B003/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلنَّعْمَةِ/قَلِيلًا`; 73:14,17 `يَوْمَ/يَوْمًا`; 73:16 `وَبِيلًا`; 73:20 `ءَاخَرُونَ/أَدْنَىٰ`","synthesis":"Reversal acts socially as well as materially: the group scatters, the first gives way to others, abundance contracts, and rank falls. This process answers the apparent permanence of luxury.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:14","73:16","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Governance, Levy, and Deterrent Liability","parent_semantic_invariant":"Rule maintains a household or polity through care, contracts, assessed transfers, testimony, and sanctions that establish responsibility.","parent_surprising_reach":"The Pharaoh precedent activates not only kingship but a full administrative frame of household allegiance, tribute, tax, covenant, wage, surety, and exemplary punishment.","key":"C","name":"Testimony establishing sanction","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A violation is established by witness, attributed to its actor, and answered with a deterrent consequence.","active_motifs":"knowledgeable testimony (`quranic:root_000822:B002/m01`); attribution of falsehood (`quranic:root_001290:B002/m01`); accountability for an offense (`quranic:root_000018:B002/m01`); deterrent punishment (`quranic:root_001554:B003/m01`); painful sanction (`quranic:root_000994:B005/m01`); harmful consequence (`quranic:root_001619:B002/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ`; 73:12 `أَنكَالًا`; 73:13 `عَذَابًا`; 73:15 `شَٰهِدًا`; 73:16 `أَخَذْنَٰهُ/وَبِيلًا`","synthesis":"Witness establishes the event, attribution identifies the violation, accountability attaches it to the actor, and sanction converts judgment into deterrence. The sequence makes punishment an institutional act rather than raw force.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:12","73:13","73:15","73:16"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Mobilization, Capture, and Mortal Outcome","parent_semantic_invariant":"Organized conflict moves from preparation and advance to close engagement, capture, subjugation, and death.","parent_surprising_reach":"The battle field includes rumor-driven mobilization, war clamor, throat holds, taming, clubs, exposure to killing, and the extinction of a group.","key":"B","name":"Capture and exposure to death","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Close engagement produces a grip, capture, bodily vulnerability, and possible extinction.","active_motifs":"arrest and captivity (`quranic:root_000018:B003/m01`); wrestling seizure (`quranic:root_000018:B011/m01`); throat grip in combat (`quranic:root_000934:B012/m01`); making someone vulnerable to killing (`quranic:root_001200:B005/m01`); extinction of a person or people (`quranic:root_001217:B009/m01`); life and soul lost at death (`quranic:root_001533:B011/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:13 `طَعَامًا`; 73:16,19 `أَخَذْنَٰهُ/أَخْذًا/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:20 `يُقَٰتِلُونَ/أَنفُسِكُم/قَرْضًا`","synthesis":"Grip becomes capture, capture creates vulnerability, and vulnerability opens onto death at both individual and collective scale. The soul motif specifies what is removed when the sequence reaches its mortal outcome.","ayah_refs":["73:13","73:16","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Mobilization, Capture, and Mortal Outcome","parent_semantic_invariant":"Organized conflict moves from preparation and advance to close engagement, capture, subjugation, and death.","parent_surprising_reach":"The battle field includes rumor-driven mobilization, war clamor, throat holds, taming, clubs, exposure to killing, and the extinction of a group.","key":"C","name":"Subjugation and the heavy instrument","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Defeated resistance is broken by force and trained into compliance.","active_motifs":"killing and removal of life (`quranic:root_001200:B001/m01`); subjugating and taming until hardness softens (`quranic:root_001200:B002/m01`); deterrent punishment (`quranic:root_001554:B003/m01`); a heavy club or beating beam (`quranic:root_001619:B004/m01`); direct striking (`quranic:root_000906:B001/m01`); transforming a person’s state (`quranic:root_000248:B002/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:12 `أَنكَالًا`; 73:16 `أَخَذْنَٰهُ/وَبِيلًا`; 73:17 `يَجْعَلُ`; 73:20 `يَضْرِبُونَ/يُقَٰتِلُونَ`","synthesis":"The outcome is not only death: force can also break resistance into trained compliance. Club, strike, transformation, and deterrence define a coercive post-capture process distinct from battlefield movement.","ayah_refs":["73:12","73:16","73:17","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Cutting, Shaping, and Material Finish","parent_semantic_invariant":"Raw material becomes a finished object through cutting, striking, grinding, weaving, covering, and surface treatment.","parent_surprising_reach":"The language of action and fulfilled promise activates an artisan workshop containing shears, adze, mallet, grinding stone, textile, pile, coating, and tanning dose.","key":"A","name":"The shaping workshop","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Workers cut and strike material, hew wood, grind solids, and weave a stable form.","active_motifs":"cutting with shears or teeth (`quranic:root_001217:B001/m01`); adze for shaping wood (`quranic:root_001207:B008/m01`); grinding stone (`quranic:root_000879:B008/m01`); workers including carpenter and digger (`quranic:root_001167:B003/m01`); heavy mallet or beating beam (`quranic:root_001619:B004/m01`); well-made weaving (`quranic:root_000217:B007/m01`); tools, striking place, and tent-making (`quranic:root_000906:B014/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:16 `وَبِيلًا`; 73:18 `مَفْعُولًا`; 73:20 `يَضْرِبُونَ/صَّلَوٰةَ/قَرْضًا/تُقَدِّمُوا`","synthesis":"Cutting establishes edges, adze and mallet shape mass, grinding reduces particles, and weaving assembles strands. Worker and tool roles make this a single production floor rather than a tool list.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:16","73:18","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Inward Self, Desire, and Breath","parent_semantic_invariant":"The self mediates intention, attraction, emotion, bodily life, and the need for relief.","parent_surprising_reach":"“Self” extends from inward purpose to breath, blood, living soul, precious competition, grief, love, anger, and release from constriction.","key":"A","name":"Inward purpose and affect","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"The self forms an intention, is drawn toward a valued object, and experiences love, grief, anger, or rivalry.","active_motifs":"inward purpose and discernment (`quranic:root_001533:B013/m01`); attraction of the self toward a thing (`quranic:root_000831:B005/m01`); love or grief felt inwardly (`quranic:root_001626:B004/m01`); anger and resentment (`quranic:root_001626:B005/m01`); precious value provoking competition (`quranic:root_001533:B010/m01`); the self characterized as deceptive (`quranic:root_001290:B008/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ`; 73:16,19 `أَخَذْنَٰهُ/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:19 `شَآءَ`; 73:20 `أَنفُسِكُم/تَجِدُوهُ`","synthesis":"Intention supplies direction, attraction supplies motive, and affect registers the result. Competition and self-deception show how perceived value can distort that inward process.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:16","73:19","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Relinquishment and Transferred Handling","parent_semantic_invariant":"Releasing an affair changes who holds, answers for, restrains, or submits it.","parent_surprising_reach":"Leaving is not a single empty gesture: it can transfer accountability to a sovereign guarantor, recoil in weakness, impose restraint, disavow an attachment, or end in submission.","key":"A","name":"Leaving an affair to accountable custody","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"One actor releases an affair while another assumes ownership, accountability, and surety for its handling.","active_motifs":"leaving a thing (`quranic:root_001638:B003/m01`); holding a person accountable for fault (`quranic:root_000018:B002/m01`); ownership and sovereignty (`quranic:root_000532:B001/m01`); surety and undertaking another’s care (`quranic:root_001332:B003/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `وَذَرْنِى`; 73:16,19 `أَخَذْنَٰهُ/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:19-20 `رَبِّهِ/رَبَّكَ`; 73:14,18,20 `كَانَتِ/كَانَ/يَكُونُ`","synthesis":"Relinquishment does not erase the affair; it moves it under a different holder. Accountability specifies judgment, sovereignty specifies authority, and surety specifies continued care, turning “leave me” into a transfer of operative control.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:16","73:19","73:14"]}],"channel_generated_outputs":{"source_dir":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073","files":[{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/channel_candidates.jsonl","role":"channel_candidates.jsonl","bytes":8914018,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/channel_candidates.tsv","role":"channel_candidates.tsv","bytes":257927,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/summary.json","role":"summary.json","bytes":8709,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/candidate_graphs.jsonl","role":"families/candidate_graphs.jsonl","bytes":4915982,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/candidate_similarity_edges.tsv","role":"families/candidate_similarity_edges.tsv","bytes":122140,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/channel_families.jsonl","role":"families/channel_families.jsonl","bytes":862645,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/candidate_family_membership.tsv","role":"families/candidate_family_membership.tsv","bytes":11610,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/family_branch_inventory.tsv","role":"families/family_branch_inventory.tsv","bytes":306169,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/consolidation_summary.json","role":"families/consolidation_summary.json","bytes":9227,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/paths/path_summary.json","role":"paths/path_summary.json","bytes":16399,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/paths/path_families/semantic_path_families.jsonl","role":"paths/path_families/semantic_path_families.jsonl","bytes":30036552,"compressed": ## V2 Rendering Profile — 5.6 Sol High

This run tests whether `5.6-sol-high` can increase lexical depth while preserving
reader-facing clarity.

Increase depth through semantic precision, not additional bulk.

Before drafting, silently build a coverage ledger:

- include every `must_integrate` topic;
- include every `candidate` with a reader payoff not already expressed;
- if `v12_reader_responses` is absent because the default workflow retired
  per-ayah focus runs, do not infer a `stage_00` isolated response or
  confidence movement;
- if `v12_focus_trace_hermetic` is present, use its `baseline_models`,
  `context_deltas`, and `surprising_valid_outliers` as reconstructed
  before/after evidence. Do not call it a staged reveal transcript. Preserve
  surprising outliers when they remain anchored in this ayah, especially
  secondary split-root activations;
- include a regular reader-walk, plus/minus-5 reader-walk, whole-surah reading,
  or cross-run-publication item only when it adds a distinct retrospective
  insight or coverage check;
- use branch inventories to support explanations; they create no standalone
  prose obligation. When fallback inventories are present, treat them as
  restricted to this ayah's roots and anchored citations, not as a whole-surah
  obligation.
- treat `root_lexicon` as full dictionary/gloss support, not branch-filtered.
  It uses QAC-to-Furuq root mapping; when a QAC root is split, non-dominant
  Furuq targets are included as additional root entries and recorded in
  coverage. If multiple QAC roots map to the same Furuq root, the shared entry
  records all source roots in `qac_roots_ar` / `qac_root_mappings`; use those
  fields for evidence attribution when present, not the legacy single `root_ar`
  alone. Dictionary/gloss branches are evidence support, not independent
  obligations, unless tied to this ayah's word, topic, reader payoff, or cited
  relation. Full field means all distinct reader payoffs from activated
  material, not every dictionary branch for the root.
- if `channel_generated_outputs` lists quran-data files and this run gives you
  file access, read only those listed files when channel-family/path detail is
  necessary. Treat them as candidate/family/path evidence, not as an adjudicated
  channel ledger. State B channel restrictions still apply.

For each critical word, preserve these distinct layers when available:

1. local grammatical work;
2. locally selected sense;
3. coherent pressure supplied by activated or cited root branches;
4. one form, sound, rarity, or variant observation with a unique payoff;
5. later contextual change, compressed to its final reader-visible result.

Do not flatten these layers into a general metaphor. Deepen generic summaries by
naming the exact lexical mechanism. Ordering grammar and local meaning first is
grounding, not truth-ranking.

At first mention of an ayah word, use a structured Arabic surface span:
`{ar:surface_form, tr:Turkish-readable transliteration, gloss:target-language meaning}`.
Use the same full span again when the prose returns to that word after moving to
another word or another paragraph. Inside one short local sequence, after a full
span has just been given, a Turkish label or transliteration is enough. Explain
every technical term in the same sentence. Keep raw roots, root skeletons,
letter-by-letter root transliterations, and branch IDs in the evidence surface.
In prose, attach root discussion to the surface word:
`{ar:ٱلْعَادِيَاتِ, tr:el-âdiyât, gloss:koşup atılanlar} kelimesinin bağlı
olduğu kök alanı...`, not `ʿ-d-v kökü...`.

A paragraph may begin with the ayah's surface, a concrete image, or a
reader-facing claim. It may not begin with a bare grammar label or stacked
abstractions. Prefer "Âyet önce hamdi Allah'a verir; sabit ad cümlesi bu hamdi
yerleşik bir hüküm olarak taşır" over "Bu âyet, tek bir isim cümlesiyle yerleşik
bir hüküm kurar."

For this short ayah, keep at least two-thirds of the prose on its own wording.
Compress all later developments into at most three paragraphs and end with one
plain synthesis paragraph.

Use `v12_cross_run_publication`, if present, only as a compact coverage/priority
check derived from regular and plus/minus-5 reader runs. Do not copy it as prose,
and do not let it override local bundle evidence.

Use an explicit negative predicate only to correct a likely misconception,
protect the primary sense from replacement, or preserve live counter-evidence.
Default ceiling for this ayah: three. Never stack two negatives in one sentence.
During final revision, rewrite all other negatives as positive predication.
Do not drive negation to zero by default: if no explicit negative is needed,
include a profile-specific style audit in friction confirming that there was no
live misconception, replacement risk, or counter-evidence requiring one.

Pass only if every paragraph has one distinct reader payoff and the prose remains
clear with the evidence surface closed.
